spec:E15-D10 — Hi-fi: SCR-027 floating panel window (Electron), its title bar and its browser build › 2. Anatomy, redlines and the measured sweep (RULED BQ-04, BQ-06, BQ-08; T Scope; T Test item 2) › 2.3 The sweep: four thresholds and the minimum width, computed (RULED BQ-08; C-i)#1	s1	**Method.** The tool sums, per step, the slot widths of §2.1 from the token values and the glyph advances of the house table — `D03T`:460–466's per-capital em bounds, `EM_SAFE` for every other glyph, `+2 %` at weight 500 and `+5 %` at weight 600, the `EM_HEADROOM` factor — read from E15-D03's tool at the base, never typed here `(heuristic)`; so the figures are **estimates built on measured token values**, which the boards confirm in PR 3 with Penpot's text bounds (G-SWEEP then compares the drawn bar to the table).

The Electron boundaries: the shell, IPC and the route (RULED BQ-21; T Sec; T OOS; cite only, nothing specified)#1	s1	The shell carries no business logic: the renderer talks to the main process through a context-isolated preload with an explicit IPC allow-list (`20`:208 B4; SR-110 … SR-113, `04`:966–969; C-12.10, `CONST`:813; `ADR11`:53), and the floating window's actions — Pin on top, Return to workspace, the redock, the display-gone notice — need main-process actions the shipped preload does not expose (`ALLOW`:11–21 lists six channels, none for a window): they are **typed preload allow-list additions for E15-S02**, one named channel per action with a typed payload behind the ShellPort, never Node, `remote` or a generic channel (E10-D03 H3, `E10D03`:854, :302; SR-111, SR-181).

spec:E15-D10 — Hi-fi: SCR-027 floating panel window (Electron), its title bar and its browser build › §C Copy-ready A-rows (PR 2 applies them byte for byte; RULED BQ-22)#1	s1	**Rules.** Old is the whole target line, verbatim, as it stands at the base inside its entry (`### SCR-027 …` in `14`, `### CMP-215 …` in `15`) — for a replace the line replaced, for an insert the line the new one follows (its anchor); New is the exact line PR 2 writes, one bullet in the entry's own form (`- **Field:**` in `14`, `- **Field**:` in `15`), each PROPOSED or "owner approval pending"; Field names the line at PR 1's base; Via is `base` for a replace (the line is the base's, written by no single PR this pack depends on) and `—` for an insert.
spec:E15-D10 — Hi-fi: SCR-027 floating panel window (Electron), its title bar and its browser build › §C Copy-ready A-rows (PR 2 applies them byte for byte; RULED BQ-22)#1	s2	The tool's PR-1 mode checks every Old against its file at the base — standing once inside its entry at the Field's number — and its PR-2 mode requires every Old to stand once inside its entry at the merge-base and each file to equal that base with §C applied exactly, so PR 2 never re-points a line by number and cannot open over a line a later PR edited.
spec:E15-D10 — Hi-fi: SCR-027 floating panel window (Electron), its title bar and its browser build › §C Copy-ready A-rows (PR 2 applies them byte for byte; RULED BQ-22)#1	s3	The export (`--a-rows`) carries each row's file, entry heading, anchor line (its Old), op and `via`, so PR 2 re-points by content.
